# Audit log

Every auditor verdict, verbatim, followed by what was done about it. Newest at the bottom.

## 2026-10-18, audit 1: before the first paid run (no spend yet), after the novelty re-scope

Context: auditor run as a general-purpose subagent with `.claude/agents/auditor.md` verbatim as instructions (the agent type was not registered when this session started), model Opus, read-only. Repo at f7bfbeb.

> VERDICT: ADJUST
>
> BUDGET: spent $0.00 of pilot $20 (anthropic $0, openai $0); last run estimate n/a vs actual n/a (no paid run; ledger empty; HEAD f7bfbeb pushed).
>
> FINDINGS:
> 1. [critical] `tasks.py` `_parse_math` parses bare text first, so math-verify keeps the last number: "(3, 2, 6)"→6, "3^{2007}"→3, predicted "2^{10}"→2. "(1, 2, 6)" passes; "2^{10}" (gold 1024) fails. Affects 93 of 1,579 Omni golds (18, 26, 40, 55 in 0-59), plus agreement and voting. Wrap in $…$ first.
> 2. [major] Omni gold 37 has unbalanced braces (ungradable). `exact` and `bbeh` reject "\*\*42\*\*", "\boxed{42}", "$12$"; the unparsed check misses this.
> 3. [major] P0 configs read `data/external/omni_math.jsonl`, which does not exist.
> 4. [major] `build_report.py` calls `gate.main("p3")` without task_ids, so P3 is judged on 0-59, not 20-59.
> 5. [major] `plan.md` leaves open: the "best candidate" and second probe; a band move that overshoots; P4 prediction 2 (best of 216 on held-out tasks); vote ≥ dense.
> 6. [major] P4 and P5 are single chunks, so `expect:` fires after all calls; banding checks nothing.
> 7. [major] Rate limits unknown: a P4 part is ~2,275 calls and 0.7–1.2M output tokens, over an hour at 10k output tokens a minute.
> 8. [major] Analysis: `random_control` has no CI (H4 primary); P4 lacks single and vote controls and the budgeted reward; agreement uses part a only; regret bands ignore task noise; `recon_error` uses a dense run that shares upstream calls.
> 9. [minor] Report and memo claim every gold parses; `log.md` lacks NEXT; 50 of 60 Omni items in 0-59 are HMMT; P5 reuses selection tasks.
>
> Clean: splits disjoint, hashes match, test slice untouched, plan precedes runs, 81 tests pass.
>
> REQUIRED BEFORE NEXT PAID RUN:
> 1. Point P0 at omni_d44_61; re-estimate; dry-run.
> 2. Before P2: fix items 1-2 with offline tests; rebuild Omni; update hashes.
> 3. Restrict the P3 gate to 20-59 in code.
> 4. Commit the item-5 rules.
> 5. Get rate limits; set concurrency and parts; chunk_size ≤ task_limit/2 with matching min_tasks; banding checks on.
> 6. Add NEXT; correct the grader claim.
>
> DIRECTION: P0 then P2 (~$0.50 worst case) is right: only it tests the premise. P2's 5-point kill on 20 items rejects a true 10-point gap about one time in three; ask Kevin whether a non-negative P2 miss should go to P3 instead of STOP. If P3's CI includes 0 or the vote matches dense, widen the gate (validation 60-119, ~$0.95) before P4's $6.30. A 10+10-task landscape cannot settle H1–H4.

**What was done** (all before any paid call; commits on `pilot_run`):

| finding / required item | fix | commit |
|---|---|---|
| 1 [critical] math answers parsed bare (last number only) | `_parse_math` parses `$…$`-wrapped LaTeX first, bare only as a fallback; regression tests for every case named (`tests/test_tasks.py`) | 3f5c4d7 |
| 2 gold 37 unbalanced; presentation not stripped | `clean_answer` (bold, ticks, `$…$`, `\boxed{}`, final period) for every grader; Omni filter adds balanced braces, non-empty LaTeX parse and no 3+-letter words (anagram matches); task files rebuilt; data cards regenerated by `scripts/write_data_cards.py` (new validation/test hashes; 100% gold self-match per set) | (same as above) |
| 3 P0 configs read a missing file | P0 configs read `omni_d44_61` items 0-4; re-estimated (`research/estimates.csv`) and dry-run | 165c861 |
| 4 P3 judged on 0-59 | `analysis/gate.py` judges P3 on positions 20-59 from the task file (20-119 with P3w); test `test_p3_gate_is_judged_on_fresh_validation_items_only` | c9c4150 |
| 5 open rules | `research/plan.md` amendment 1: probe order, P3 candidate choice, band overshoot, P3w trigger, P4 go/no-go against the propose vote, P4 sizing from rate limits, P4 cost check, HMMT probe, P5 tasks; the P2-kill power question goes to Kevin | 0ce98aa |

## 2026-10-18, audit 2: verification of audit 1 and review of the conclusions (no spend yet)

Context: `auditor` agent (now registered), read-only, repo at 11d1c02.

> VERDICT: ADJUST
>
> BUDGET: spent $0.00 of the pilot's $20 (anthropic $0, openai $0). Last run: no paid run yet, so no estimate-vs-actual. Ledger is empty; HEAD 11d1c02 matches origin/pilot_run; working tree clean.
>
> FINDINGS:
> 1. [major] The grader in `autoagentnet/tasks.py` can still mark wrong answers right. I probed it directly.
>    - (a) `math_equal` falls back to string comparison through `normalize()` even when both sides parse, and `normalize()` deletes commas. Gold "n=1,2" (item 38, which the P3 gate judges) matches "n=12". Golds 98 and 117 match their digits run together. Gold "12" matches "1,2".
>    - (b) When the `$…$` parse fails, the bare parse keeps the last number: "(1, 2, 6" grades as 6, "{5, 7" as 7, "5 (or possibly 7)" as 7, and "$5$ or $7$" as 7.
>    - `max_unparsed_rate` only counts missing ANSWER lines, so none of this would show up.
>    - The gold side is clean: all 1,557 golds parse as LaTeX and match themselves, and the structured golds in 0-119 grade correctly.
> 2. [major] `reports/pilot/report.pdf`, `report.html` and `tables/values.json` were built at 6a23efb (02:08), before the audit-1 fixes. The PDF still says "math-verify parses every Omni gold answer" and gives a $8.53 total. The audit log marks that claim as fixed.
> 3. [major] Plan amendment rules 5 and 6 let P4's $6.33 go ahead if dense beats the six-answer propose vote by a single item (2.5 points at n=40). One item is 2.5 points, so "within 2 points" triggers P3w only when the vote ties or beats dense. Voting explaining the gap is the likeliest failure (2508.17536), and this decision carries the most money.
> 4. [major] The decision memo says "take B if the voting-check gain is ≥3 points". That is the wrong quantity: rule 6 is about dense minus vote. Option B also spends the untouched test slice on picks chosen on 10 tasks, which P4 prediction 4 says cannot resolve H1.
> 5. [minor] Stale or overstated numbers in the memo and report:
>    - The memo says "81 tests"; 110 pass now.
>    - $38.17, $5.90 and $96 come from the stale values.json; `scaleup_costs.csv` now gives $37.99, $5.84 and $95.82.
>    - 625 configs can still be enumerated, so it is not "the space where a learned controller could beat enumeration".
>    - The report's "SE 6-7 points" at n=40 does not match `power.csv`, which gives 7.1-8.7.
> 6. [minor] Gaps in NEXT and the analysis scripts:
>    - NEXT does not say to re-estimate and dry-run after regenerating configs (P0 ref change, `--p4-parts`).
>    - `p0_dense_<ref>` has to be written by hand.
>    - `build_report` splits P4 parts into search and held-out halves by count, so a partial P4 labels part b as held out.
>    - `gate.judged_task_ids` silently falls back to all items if the config name differs.
> 7. [minor] The HMMT contamination probe has only 6 non-HMMT judged items (18 with P3w), so it cannot detect recall. Items 90 (a yes/no gold) and 115 (gold is an inequality) are weak.
>
> Clean:
> - 110 tests pass.
> - Split hashes recomputed and match; no duplicate prompts; banding pool disjoint; no position ≥120 used.
> - P0 configs read `omni_d44_61` items 0-4.
> - Estimates for all 20 configs postdate the data rebuild.
> - P3 is judged on 20-59, or 20-119 with P3w, and a test covers it.
> - Rate limits from both providers reach `status.json`.
> - The new analysis pieces (random-control CI, single and vote baselines, budgeted picks, pooled agreement) are tested.
> - P2-P4 chunking lets a bad run stop part-way.
>
> REQUIRED BEFORE NEXT PAID RUN:
> 1. Grader:
>    - only fall back to string comparison when a side fails to parse, and keep commas;
>    - count a bare-fallback prediction as unparsed;
>    - add the cases above to `tests/test_tasks.py`.
> 2. Commit to the plan before P2: P4 runs only if dense minus vote6 is at least 5 points on the judged items, with the paired CI reported.
> 3. NEXT: re-estimate and dry-run after any regeneration; split P4 halves by part name.
> 4. Before the report goes to Kevin: rebuild `report.pdf`, fix the memo's numbers, and change the condition for Option B.
>
> DIRECTION: P0 then the P2 Omni probe (about $0.50 worst case) is still the cheapest test of the premise. The biggest open question is whether dense beats a plain vote over the six propose answers (Q1c/Q2b). The P2 and P3 dense runs answer it at no extra cost, so make it the real P4 gate. If the margin is under 5 points, report "voting explains the gap" and save the $6.33. After P4, check the picks on fresh validation items 60-119 rather than the test slice. Dense and single are already cached there from P3w, so only the pruned configs cost anything: about $1.7 at the test-picks rate, against $5.90 for Option B. The test slice then stays clean for the full study.

**What was done** (all before any paid call):

| finding / required item | fix | commit |
|---|---|---|
| 1 / R1 grader fallbacks | predictions are parsed with math-verify's LaTeX extractor only (the default expression extractor recovered the last number of malformed text even inside `$…$`); answers with several `$…$` segments do not parse; display-math wrappers are unwrapped; string comparison only when the *gold* fails to parse; commas removed only as thousands separators; `answer_parsed` makes an unparseable math answer count as unparsed (so `max_unparsed_rate` catches format trouble); Omni filter also drops yes/no and inequality golds; task files and data cards rebuilt (100% of the 1,536 kept golds parse as LaTeX); regression tests for every case listed | 97ce7fd |
| 3 / R2 P4 go/no-go | amendment 2, rule 11: P4 only if dense beats single and the 6-answer vote by ≥ 5 points each on the judged items, with paired CIs (`gate.py` now reports `dense_minus_vote6` with a bootstrap CI; tested); rule 12 re-states the P3w trigger | c35db17 |
| 4 Option B / test slice | amendment 2, rule 13: picks are confirmed on validation 60-119 ($0.82 worst case, picks only; `scaleup_costs.csv`); the memo's options are now A (pilot incl. that confirmation), B (power-first confirmation on 600 untouched items, $17.65, MDE ≈ 5 points), C (full landscape, $38.04) | c35db17 |
| 5 stale numbers | memo rewritten from the regenerated tables (10-task measurement in `scaleup_costs.py`); 4×5 wording corrected; power stated from `power.csv` (7-9 points SE at n=40; table in the report); report rebuilt after all fixes | c35db17 |
| 6 / R3 NEXT and scripts | NEXT: re-estimate and dry-run after any regeneration; generator writes P0 configs incl. `p0_dense_<ref>`; P4 halves split by configured part letter (tested); `judged_task_ids` raises instead of falling back | c35db17 |
| 7 HMMT probe power | amendment 2, rule 14 records that the probe cannot detect recall at this n; weak golds (yes/no, inequalities) dropped | 97ce7fd, c35db17 |
| R4 report | report.pdf/html and values.json rebuilt after every fix above | c35db17 |

## 2026-10-18, audit 3: confirmation of the audit-2 fixes, run while protocol v2 was being merged (no spend yet)

Context: `auditor` agent, read-only. Requested at 7e7e0f4 (session 1, end); HEAD moved to the v2 merge during the audit, and amendment 3 was still uncommitted, so the verdict also covers the v2 integration.

> VERDICT: ADJUST
>
> BUDGET: spent $0.00 of the pilot's $20 (Anthropic $0, OpenAI $0). No paid run yet, so there is no estimate-vs-actual. HEAD moved during this audit, from 7e7e0f4 to d2f832f (the v2 merge); it still matches origin/pilot_run. Amendment 3 and the regenerated configs are uncommitted.
>
> FINDINGS:
> 1. [minor] Grader (`autoagentnet/tasks.py`): every audit-2 case is now graded correctly, and 133 tests pass. Three gaps remain:
>    - "1\,250" and "1\ 250" parse as 251. These count as parsed but are graded wrong, and 21 of the golds in positions 0-119 are integers of 4+ digits.
>    - A `\boxed{}` anywhere in the answer decides it: "\boxed{5} or 7" grades as 5.
>    - Prose answers ("The answer is 5", "approximately 5") parse as products of letters. They count as parsed, so rule 15's unparsed check misses them.
> 2. [major] v2 reopened the confirmation design. Committed rule 13 confirms on 60-119, but the v2 P4 held-out half is 60-99, and `docs/PILOT_PROMPT.md` cites only amendment 1. Draft amendment 3 fixes this, with two gaps:
>    - P3w (60-119) overlaps the held-out half, P5 (60-69) and `judge_cascade`, so a widened gate is judged on held-out items.
>    - Sonnet within 5 points of dense means "P4 may run": the choice is left open.
> 3. [major] `build_report.p4_halves` only finds `p4_*_landscape_?` folders, so it would silently skip the new `p4_*_grid_*` parts. `gate.py` has no `futile` column (draft rule 19). `judged_task_ids` raising and `dense_minus_vote6` with its CI are correct and tested.
> 4. [major] NEXT step 8 still runs the superseded 216-config P4, and NEXT has no prereg or `aan check` step.
> 5. [major] Report, memo and tables agree with each other, but:
>    - Prices use the old estimator; `aan estimate` now gives about 17% more (`p3_omni_dense` $1.07 vs $0.915).
>    - Option B's total of $41 holds (about $40.7 needed).
>    - Option C's $65 covers pilot + C (about $64.5), not the memo's recommended A→B→C order (about $75.7 at old prices).
>    - Option C is the 216-config landscape that v2 replaced.
>    - Report §7 says amendment 1 settles every open rule.
>
> REQUIRED BEFORE NEXT PAID RUN:
> 1. Commit amendment 3 with P3w limited to 100-119, so 60-99 stays clean, and a fixed Sonnet rule.
> 2. Make `p4_halves` find the grid parts, add `futile` to `gate.py`, and add tests for both.
> 3. Grader: remove thin-space separators between digit groups, require the whole answer to be boxed, count runs of 3+ letters as unparsed, and add tests.
> 4. Re-record estimates, dry-run, run `aan check` on every changed config, and rewrite NEXT.
> 5. Before the memo goes to Kevin: rewrite the options for v2 at current prices, with C's total covering pilot + B + C.
>
> DIRECTION: The premise is untested. P0 (about $0.10), then the P2 Omni probe, is still the cheapest test. The draft's own P3 prediction expects dense minus vote6 below 5 points. So the decision that matters is whether dense beats the vote and single Sonnet (P3, about $1.6), and that should be settled before any money goes to the $6.46 grid.

## 2026-10-18, audit 4: confirmation of the audit-3 fixes and review of the v2 report and memo (no spend yet)

Context: `auditor` agent, read-only, at a5c91da (session 2). Kevin merged `pilot_run` into `main` (PR #4) while the verdict was being acted on, so the fixes below land on a fresh `pilot_run` from `main`.

> VERDICT: ADJUST
>
> BUDGET: spent $0.00 of the pilot's $20 (Anthropic $0, OpenAI $0). No paid run yet, so there is no estimate-vs-actual. HEAD a5c91da matches origin/pilot_run, the tree is clean and the ledger is empty. `python -m pytest -q`: 151 passed. The pilot's worst case is $10.84, from the latest rows in `research/estimates.csv` at 3 characters per token. `aan check` printed `cleared` on all 8 configs I sampled.
>
> FINDINGS:
> 1. [major] The 3-letter rule (`autoagentnet/tasks.py:113`) over-reaches.
>    - It grades correct answers with unit words as wrong and unparsed: "14040 \text{ ways}" (gold 5), "12 \text{ days}", "30 \text{ degrees}". math-verify on its own grades all of these right.
>    - Prose answers were already graded wrong before the rule, so the rule only flags them as unparsed. Its only effect on accuracy is the units loss.
>    - The prompts never forbid units, and one proposer is told to "Track units".
>    - Risks: lower accuracy, the 0.10 unparsed stop firing in every config, and skewed votes. No test covers this.
> 2. [minor] `THOUSANDS_RE` merges tuples: "(2,100)" grades equal to 2100. Golds 181 (test slice), 884 (B range) and 1265 are mangled, so a correct "1, 13, 43, 91, 157" fails. No gold in 0-119 is affected. The filter also keeps 21 `\text{No}` golds and 2 "OR" golds, against audit 2's "drops yes/no" row.
> 3. [major] The memo prices option B on placeholder picks (`scaleup_costs.PICKS`).
>    - Review-heavy picks ([1,6,6,1], [3,6,6,1], [1,3,6,1]) cost $27.75, not $16.58 (my offline estimate). B is then about $34 and pilot + B about $54, not $43.
>    - The rest of the arithmetic holds: $22.63 / $37.86 / $17.91; totals $43 / $58 / $61 / $76; at most about $75 on Anthropic.
> 4. [major] The scale-up triggers have almost no power at n=40.
>    - The p4b prereg's "within dense's 95% CI" accepts a policy about 15 points worse than dense.
>    - "A pick or policy beats random pruning and vote6" across about 8 designs is a multiple-comparison go.
>    - These triggers release $22.63 to $37.86.
> 5. [minor] θ=0.67 excludes 2/3 (0.6667 < 0.67) in `economics.py:179` and `designs.py:71`. Only 10 of the 16 (m, θ) policies are distinct, so 2-of-3 and 4-of-6 are never scored.
> 6. [minor] Report and plan wording:
>    - Report §3 misstates rule 18.
>    - "Every design is scored on the same held-out items" is false for Sonnet, which is scored on 20-59.
>    - `report.pdf` predates a5c91da.
>    - `plan.md` Steps table and question tree are still v1.
>    - The `futile=True` path is untested.
> 7. Clean:
>    - all 29 preregs are verbatim copies of the plan block;
>    - offsets match rules 20-23; `JUDGED_POSITIONS` and `p4_halves` are correct;
>    - every choice is made on the search half only, and the paired CIs are aligned;
>    - the fallback proposer-reuse cost is correct;
>    - the cascade's proposers are identical to dense's;
>    - the memo's phase formula matches `autoagentnet/budget.py`.
>
> REQUIRED BEFORE NEXT PAID RUN (P0 may run first, since its decision does not depend on grading; the rest before P2 and before the PR goes to Kevin):
> 1. Let units through (`\text{…}` and words after a number) while keeping prose unparsed, with tests. Or have the prompts ask for the number only.
> 2. Apply separator removal only to a lone number, and fix the yes/no and "OR" filter before any B or C run.
> 3. Memo: give B as a cost range that depends on the picks, and make `scaleup_costs.py` read P4's picks.
> 4. Give H5 a fixed margin (for example at least −3 points at ≤50% cost). Name one pre-chosen design for each trigger, and commit both before P4b.
> 5. Set θ to 2/3, fix the wording in finding 6, and rebuild the report.
>
> DIRECTION: The premise is still untested. P0 ($0.10), the P2 Omni probe ($0.36) and P3 ($1.84) remain the cheapest test of the decisive question: does dense beat vote6 and one Sonnet call? P0's real outputs are also the cheapest evidence on how Haiku formats its ANSWER line, so check the unparsed reasons there before P2. P5 ($0.54) adds little beyond P0's `luna_low` calibration, because 10 items cannot order the picks; make it conditional.

## 2026-10-19, audit 10: after the d61_78 banding stopped on unparsed answers (session 4)

Context: `auditor` subagent, read-only, repo at 84b11c3.

> VERDICT: ADJUST
>
> BUDGET: spent $0.148 of the $20 pilot (anthropic $0.141, openai $0.007). The last run's estimate was $0.0465 for 25 items, about $0.019 pro rata for the 10 it did; actual was $0.0069 (0.37x). The prereg (c2739f9) and the estimate (fca5420) were both committed before the run (e3f8be3). The tree is clean and HEAD 84b11c3 matches origin/pilot_run.
>
> FINDINGS:
> 1. [major] I read all 10 raw outputs, and every grade checks out except omni-4089, which the grader missed. Hand grade: 6/10. Option 3 (unfreezing the grader) would not fix that item. I tested the matcher offline: `answers_match("f(x) = \frac{1}{x + a}", "f(x) = 1/(x+c)", "math")` returns False even with the qualifier removed. The real cause is the renamed free parameter, as with omni-3963 in audit 9.
> 2. [major] New: the vote6 control is biased on this window. `autoagentnet/consensus.py` groups proposers with `answers_match`, so "1/(x+c)" and "1/(x+k)" count as different answers and split the vote, while dense's aggregator writes a single string.
>    - Effect: on d61 this pushes dense minus vote6 (rule 11) up and the plurality shares used for H5 and H6 down.
>    - Exposure: of items 0-119, 35 have several-part golds, and 4 have golds with a parameter the problem never defines: omni-4153, omni-4168, omni-3634 and omni-4259 (the last is in calibration).
> 3. [major] The 10% unparsed stop will come back. With 20 final answers per arm, 3 unparsed answers stop P2b. Relaxing it for banding only just moves the same question to P2b.

Blind hand grading removes d61's grading noise at no cost. Nothing removes AIME's contamination, which squeezes the gap and inflates vote6, so a STOP on AIME would be hard to read. AIME stays the pre-registered fallback (amendment rule 1) if banding drops Omni or P2b fails. The open question is whether a Haiku team at about 35% of Sonnet's cost stays within a few points of it on fresh items. Parts i-p plus held-out Sonnet answer that for about $2.4 plus $0.56, for a projected pilot total of about $6.7. That is the cheapest decisive step. Dense runs live on held-out items, so expect about $0.30 per part, and keep $0.42 as the stop for every part, not only part i. Keep the rule 62 band check and the audits after parts l and p. Leave the cascade conditional on H5 still being open after part p: the free vote-or-Sonnet row largely covers it. Running the picks only on held-out would save about $1.7, but it would drop the held-out landscape and the critique/refine "<2 points per doubling" decision in p4_grid. With $13 of headroom, keep the preregistered full grid.

**What was done:** plan rule 65 before part i: prediction (1) and the production function are judged on all 80 items; the held-out comparisons are reported by task; the $0.42 stop applies to every part; after part p the picks are checked with `git diff` against 13d5cfd; the cascade is conditional on H5 being open after part p.

Kevin's control_resample gate is not met, and it lives only in prose, not in code.
>
> FINDINGS:
> 1. [major] M4 cannot resolve its 10-point threshold with 2 sessions. I ran a free ZI-C null in the scratchpad (same engine, 300 A/B pairs). The gap has an SD of 12.2 points and P(gap ≥ 10) = 17%, with a one-sided 95th percentile of 19.3. The 22-rank-pair bootstrap interval excludes 0 in 10.7% of null pairs, against a nominal 5%, because it ignores session-to-session variance. Even so, plan.md section 7 turns this result into a design rule for the agent company.
> 2. [major] The M4 gap mixes two things: who gets to trade, and at what price. If Sonnet waits (MT2), it loses profit even when it wins on price, and no breakdown of the gap is preregistered.
> 3. [major] M3 "holds" on point estimates alone. With ZI-C noise, 2 sessions come out lower than 4 on both efficiency and trades by chance 29% of the time; the 95% null difference is 6.5 points. "Waiting" is never given a measurable definition.
> 4. [minor] Parser (`ACTION_RE`, /home/user/AutoAgentNetwork/autoagentnet/market/traders.py):
>    - "ACTION: \*\*BID 1.85\*\*" and "\*\*BID\*\* 1.85" come back unparsed.
>    - "BID 1,85" is read as $1.00.
>    - The unparsed and truncation stops run only at the end of a period, so a Sonnet format failure costs up to 176 calls and loses the session.
> 5. [minor] The prompt says a buyer "may not pay more than its value", but `engine.py:143` rejects any bid above value, even one that would trade at a lower ask. This pushes up the rejection rate that M1 predicts will stay under 10%.
> 6. [minor] Cost checks:
>    - The worst case counts 3 characters per token. Ledger rows 26 and 469 show 1.22 to 1.42x that. At 1.33-1.5x, M3's true worst case is $3.89-4.16 before the safety multiplier ($4.47-4.78 after it), so the plan's "R = 8 fits" overstates. The governor and the zero-trade stop still protect the money.
>    - The "1.5x the worst-case estimate" kill can never fire for M3 or M4. Nothing compares spend with the $2.2-3.2 projection or with Kevin's $5-8.
> 7. [minor] m2_haiku.yaml passes the gate with all 4 sessions in one run, which would take about 1.5-2 hours. The NEXT section of research/log.md still describes the pilot.
>
> Checked and correct: schedule, P*, Q*, maximum surplus 7.50, alpha, extramarginal trades (rank above 6), CE profit, and ZI-C 90.36 (SD 3.84) with thresholds 85.4 / 80.4. Seeds and cache keys are independent. 20 of 20 tests pass, and an interrupted run replays its cached calls for free.
>
> REQUIRED BEFORE NEXT PAID RUN:
> 1. M4: count exploitation only if the gap is at least 20 points and larger than every placebo gap from M2's all-Haiku sessions (label odd and even ranks; free). Report the rank-pair interval as descriptive only. Preregister the breakdown of the gap: intramarginal trade rate by model, and price advantage in Sonnet-Haiku trades. No design rule from 2 sessions.
> 2. M3: "holds" requires efficiency at least 7 points below M2 and fewer trades. Define waiting, for example as the PASS share of untraded intramarginal traders in rounds 6-8, plus the final spread.
> 3. Parser: strip `*`, `_` and backticks, and treat comma decimals as unparsed. Check the rates after every round. Add fake-model tests.
> 4. Rules text: "may not bid or pay more than its value; may not ask or sell below its cost".
> 5. After M1: log the real-to-estimated token ratio and the cost per turn, re-project M2-M4, and preregister a cumulative market stop at $8.
> 6. Set m2 `max_usd` to 0.25 so the gate forces one session per run. Regenerate the configs, commit, and rerun `aan check`.
>
> DIRECTION: Once the gate opens, M1 ($0.08 worst case) is the most informative dollar: it measures parse rate, cache reads, the token ratio and cost per turn for the real prompts. M2 is the core sanity check. Then run M4 before M3. M4 answers the question the project cares about (seniors against juniors), costs about half as much per session, and tests Sonnet's reply format on half the seats first. M3's scale question is confounded with R = 8, so it is the first thing to drop if the re-projection after M1 points past $8. More M4 sessions only make sense if the 20-point bar looks reachable.

**What was done** (all free, before any paid call):

| item | action |
|---|---|
| 1 / required 1 | M4 decision: exploitation only at a gap of at least 20 points and above every placebo gap from M2's sessions (`metrics.placebo_gaps`, 12 ordered pairs); the rank-pair interval (`metrics.rank_pair_interval`) is descriptive only; breakdown preregistered and coded (`metrics.senior_breakdown`: trade rate by model, price edge); no design rule from 2 sessions. The session re-ran the ZI-C null with the new code: gap SD 10.5, P(gap >= 10) 17%, P(gap >= 20) 3.3%, 95th percentile 17.6, rank-pair interval excludes 0 in 6.7% |
| 2, 3 / required 2 | M3 "holds" only at efficiency at least 7 points below M2 and fewer trades (ZI-C null: 2.8% chance; both lower 28%); waiting = late PASS share (rounds 6-8, traders with a positive CE profit) and final spread (`metrics.waiting_metrics`), in the M3 prediction |
| 4 / required 3 | parser strips `*`, `_` and backticks, and reads "1,85" as unparsed; the unparsed and truncation stops run after every round (`run_session(after_round=...)`); tests added (a broken format now stops after 44 turns) |
| 5 / required 4 | rules text: "A buyer may not bid or pay more than its value, and a seller may not ask or sell below its cost"; M3's worst case is now $3.367 ($3.872 with safety) |
| 6, 7 / required 5 | kill on spend now compares with the re-projection after M1; cumulative market stop at $8 (M3 dropped first); the plan states the 1.22-1.5x token ratio and the true Sonnet worst case of about $3.9-4.2 |

## 2026-10-20, audit 40: before the first W2-A call (the desk smoke test)

Auditor verdict, verbatim:

> VERDICT: ADJUST
>
> BUDGET: spent $9.6593 of pilot $20 (anthropic $9.6524, openai $0.0069). The last run, w0_haiku5, was estimated at $0.98 and cost $0.2785. The smoke test has not run yet: worst case $0.0051 (recorded), projected $0.003. Haiku calls in the market town cost $0.00007 each, so the $0.0045 kill line is safe. The tree is clean and origin matches HEAD (9dc3f50).
>
> FINDINGS:
> 1. [minor] The generator is correct.
>    - The Kelly formulas derive correctly.
>    - Over 20,000 events, corr(y − true p, q − true p) = 0.005, and the item's side correlates 0.004.
>    - The sealed-block statistics reproduce the plan: median |k| 0.172, full Kelly 0.0235, half Kelly 0.0156.
>    - The template hashes match, the prompts use placeholders only, and S1-S4 match program.md. Tell Kevin that S5 is deferred.
> 2. [major] RULES in /home/user/AutoAgentNetwork/autoagentnet/desk/prompts.py never say how q is formed, or that only the clues are evidence. plan_w2.md line 41 claims they do. As written, deferring to q or to the named witness is a reasonable choice, yet it gets scored as belief error and as salience.
> 3. [major] One losing STAKE of 1 makes S1's log growth −inf (metrics.py:79). Every structure then passes rule 1's growth test, and the intervals become NaN. Even without that, growth differences carry about ±0.008 at n=1,000, so "at least S1's point estimate" is close to a coin flip.
> 4. [major] There is no stay-out benchmark. Staking nothing has a sizing error of 0.223 on the sealed block. A structure whose p simply copies q could therefore become "the recommended design".
> 5. [major] The item's side is a fair coin. Its pull raises stakes on aligned events and lowers them on opposed ones, so the unsigned effect in rule 2 cancels out. The plan's own smallest detectable effect (0.02-0.04) is also larger than its predicted effect (0-0.02).
> 6. [minor] The anchoring slope is confounded: the fake never reads q, yet it gets a slope of −0.20. Regress on q's noise term instead.
> 7. [minor] Smaller fixes:
>    - wrong_side counts bets with zero stake.
>    - The 5% kill rule pools members 2-6, so one failing member can hide.
>    - decision_lines prints "replicates", which contradicts the plan.
>    - w2a_sonnet_101 has max_usd $2.10, below the $2.32 its gate needs.
>    - On sealed events with an item, the item matches the outcome only 46% of the time (r=−0.105), so read salience on true-p measures only.
>    - Missing: the pointer in plan.md, the log entry, and recorded estimates for tune and judge.
>    - The plan should say that fixing a prompt after any sealed run means new seeds.
>
> REQUIRED BEFORE NEXT PAID RUN:
> 1. Before the smoke test: add to RULES that the base rate and clues are the only evidence, and that the market prices them with noise and knows nothing more. Alternatively, retract line 41 and reword belief error. Then update the hashes, add a test, dry-run and commit.
> 2. Before w2a_judge_101, preregister:
>    - how −inf log growth is handled;
>    - a non-inferiority margin for log growth;
>    - a stay-out benchmark the recommended design must beat;
>    - the salience effect split by whether the item agrees with the true-Kelly side, plus stake_pull.
> 3. Fix the items in finding 7.
>
> DIRECTION: W2-A is the cheapest open question with known truth (about $1.3), and it does not depend on the weakened team premise. Use w2a_tune as a real pilot, not only a format check. Preregister checks there that can still change the prompts before the seal: bet rate, share of stakes at 1, share with |p−q|<0.02, and anchoring. If any of them fails, the sealed block would tell us little. For salience, the best gain per dollar is a twin trader call without the item on each sealed event that carries one (about 500 calls, about $0.05). That turns rule 2 into a paired test on the same event.

**What was done (before the smoke test):**

| finding | action |
|---|---|
| 2 | RULES now say that the base rate and the clues are the only evidence, and how the market forms its price (rules hash `58f509f1e8a50966`); test added |
| 3 | per-event growth floored at 0.01 (log growth finite; ruin read from the unfloored path); rule 1(b) is a non-inferiority margin, the lower 95% bound of the log-growth difference above -0.005; tests added |
| 4 | stay-out benchmark (sizing error 0.223, log growth 0); rule 1(c): the recommended design must beat it |
| 5 | twin trader call without the item on every event that has one (structure S1_twin); rule 2 is now paired: 2a the stake, 2b stake_pull; the stake effect is split by agreement with the Kelly side; power restated (about 0.125 x SD of the difference) |
| 6 | anchoring is the slope of (p - true p) on (q - fair price), the price's noise term only; test added |
| 7 | wrong_side needs a positive stake; the 5% rule is per role (each committee member alone), from 100 replies per role (10 in the smoke test); decision lines say "consistent with", never "replicates"; Sonnet run cap $2.40; estimates recorded for tune, judge and Sonnet; pointer rule 74 in research/plan.md; a prompt changed after any sealed run voids the sealed runs and restarts on sequences 106-110 |
| direction | the tuning block is a real pilot with preregistered checks that can still change the trader prompt (bet rate, all-in share, p copying q, anchoring) |

## 2026-10-22, audit 50: session E3 after the alignment with Kevin's brief ($0 spent)

Verdict, verbatim:

> VERDICT: ADJUST
>
> BUDGET: spent $11.13 of the $20 pilot (anthropic $11.12, openai $0.007). There was no paid run, so there is no estimate-vs-actual to compare. `state/`, budget.yaml and pricing.yaml are untouched. The branch is pushed and clean at 14474e1, and no commit carries an attribution trailer.
>
> FINDINGS:
> 1. [major] Kevin's aggregator breaks ties differently depending on the order the calls are listed in. It is fitted on ties sent to the class met first (`autoagentnet/pruning/replay.py:400-409`) but predicts with ties sent to the class the model scores highest (`:486-488`). As a result, the first-listed source's coefficient merges with the intercept (beta ≈ 0). The later source's coefficient becomes the "agreement bonus", so ties go to whichever source is listed later. I checked this on the cached 80 items:
>    - haiku:0+sonnet:0 is 72.5% cross-fitted with Haiku listed first, but 56.2% with Sonnet first (in-sample 52.5%).
>    - Averaged over the 63 sub-networks: ridge 62.5% → 61.0%, plain vote 57.2% → 62.5%.
>    - So "+5.2 over the plain vote" (`phase3a.md:7`) and the count-vector best of 72.5% are artifacts of the order. With the order reversed, Kevin's aggregator is 1.5 points *below* the plain vote.
>    - The 3B panel lists Sonnet and Sol before the persona units (`analysis/pruning_select.py:80-85`), so a Sonnet + persona network gives every tie to the persona call.
>    - The only test (`tests/test_pruning_library.py:82-98`) checks the favourable order. Deviations #2-3 present this tie-break as a feature.
> 2. [major] `deviations.md` is incomplete. Checked against `kevin_brief.md`:
>    - S4 regret is measured against the 63-subset frontier, not the call-count-vector frontier the brief names (`pruning_phase3a.py:278`).
>    - Tokens are never reported as a resource.
>    - Static vs dynamic is never compared at equal average dollars.
>    - "Anthropic half before Oct 31" cannot happen, because the selection needs the `*_open` runs. This is only in the log's NEXT list.
>    - The proposed lockbox gate is a change to Kevin's plan.
>    - The N:M per-round quotas are not built.
>    - #12 says switching the aggregator is "one line (`refit` in analysis/pruning_select.py)". No such line exists: the choice sits in the defaults of `s2_netadapt`, `pooled`, `Prefixes` and `fit`.
> 3. [minor] Stale text after the regeneration:
>    - `phase3a.md:184` (hard-coded at `pruning_phase3a.py:824-830`) says step 4 differs between the halves and that "noise drives the steps where their networks differ". The new table (`phase3a.md:177-182`) has identical networks at every step.
>    - `pruning_select.py:30,35,45` still say "weights".
> 4. [minor] `pruning_select.py:209`: `or str(full_path["kept"].iloc[-1])` is a silent fallback. It breaks the no-fallback rule and `selection_rule.md` step 4.
> 5. [minor] Power checks:
>    - Fine: void studies are excluded, NaN is demoted, and the decision uses the base case at the Holm floor (`pruning_power.py:158-162`).
>    - "Power at 0" is valid arithmetic, but under rule (a) about 0.33 of 0.63 live P1 studies freeze Sol alone, so P1 is mostly a Sol-vs-Sonnet comparison.
>    - Every static pick contains Sonnet or Sol (has_mid 0.93 + none 0.07), and Sol costs $0.019, so no simulated pick is ever cheaper than Sonnet.
>    - The demotion of P1-P3 holds either way.
> 6. [minor] On individual calls, C1-C3b fit one feature per call (`criteria.py:73-83`). The paths and C6 refit with one feature per source (`replay.py:436`), so these criteria score a model that is never used. Their ranks among exchangeable Haiku calls are noise (`phase3a.md:131-135`).
> 7. [minor] Style: `pruning_phase3a.py:345-356` loops over a dict of lists. This predates c5eb648.
>
> Checked and fine:
> - 44 tests pass.
> - "restrict" is the dense coefficients with the pruned terms dropped, which is correct.
> - Unparsed (invalid) classes can never win a tie (count key −1, masked by `has_class`).
> - No held-out rows leak into NetAdapt, `pooled`, `dynamic_table`, `pick_tau`, S1 or CEM.
> - The Summary's numbers match the tables: 75.0%/1.10x, 72.5%/1.05x, +5.2, C5 −0.79, 13 of 19, phi 0.08/0.44.
> - The power formula's sign and the P1-P3 void logic are right.
>
> REQUIRED BEFORE NEXT PAID RUN (items 1-3 before the G2a read; nothing here blocks the Phase 1 probes):
> 1. Make the tie-break independent of call order. Either fit on every tied top class (each row weighted 1/number tied), or iterate fit → re-pick until it is stable. Add a test that shuffling the call order leaves accuracy unchanged. Then regenerate 3A and `power.md`.
> 2. Correct the Summary's aggregator claim and the stale lines, and complete `deviations.md`.
> 3. Remove the fallback at line 209 (make the test void instead).
> 4. Kevin decides the lockbox gate before any e31 spend, including whether a single-call static pick (Sonnet or Sol) voids P1.
>
> DIRECTION: The power simulation already answers 3B's main question. Under the rule as written, the static selection never costs less than Sonnet. Under the 0.8x bound it selects nothing. So about $13.5 of lockbox spend would mostly test Sonnet or Sol against Sonnet and change no decision. The most informative step per dollar is still Phase 1 e13, once Kevin lands the prices. Its Haiku-Luna error correlation decides whether a cheap cross-vendor network can exist at all. Fix the aggregator first ($0), because 3B selects on it. Fund the lockbox only if the frozen selection contains something cheaper than Sonnet, and centre it on dynamic, cascade-style policies: in 3A they are the only thing that reaches Sonnet's accuracy for less (the 3-of-5 cascade: 71.2% at 0.57x).

> 1. AH2 (confirmatory; passes Holm at 5%, p = 0.001). Items: 440 BBEH items (linguini, nycc, word sorting, causal understanding) and 93 arXivMath problems. Dollars are measured at list prices.
>    - Raising a cheap model's reasoning one step beat repeating its no-reasoning call at the nearest measured dollars.
>    - Haiku: one effort-low call ($0.00077 per item) beat the Haiku-off 2-vote ($0.00066) by +3.7 points (95% item-bootstrap interval 1.3 to 6.2).
>    - Luna: one low call ($0.00036) beat the Luna-none 3-vote ($0.00026) by +10.7 (7.1 to 14.2).

>    - Haiku low's math calls come from the 12,288-token fallback; 9.3% were truncated and count as wrong.

>    - Auditor post-read: the effect sits mainly in word sorting (and linguini for Luna).
>    - Decision as frozen: the paper's budget rule puts the first effort step before width for cheap models on these items. It does not cover higher effort steps or other tasks.
> 2. AH3 (confirmatory; passes).
>    - Cross-fitted on single calls, the factor model predicted the six 3-call arms' 3-vote accuracy with a mean absolute error (MAE) of 0.8 points (largest 1.5; upper bound 2.4).
>    - It predicted their gain over one call with an MAE of 0.7 (upper bound 1.3). Both are smaller than P2's predictions of 2 and 1.
>    - Gains were small (0.4 to 2.6), and a zero-gain predictor also meets all four limits. So the test shows accuracy when vote gains are small, not that the model captures composition.
>    - The model missed Sol low's math gain (+7.9 observed, +0.4 predicted), which comes mostly from abstentions dropping out of the vote.
>    - Not covered: mixed panels, votes above 3, Haiku high, Sol medium, and items without single-call data.
>    - Decision as frozen: the Atlas may price same-arm votes of up to 3 calls. Mixed panels stay untested.
> 3. AH1 (exploratory; power 0.00).
>    - Sonnet's effort-low setting closed none of the 11.1-point gap to Sol low (R = 0.02, −0.32 to 0.26).
>    - Low vs off: +0.2 overall (−2.8 to 3.0), +1.1 on BBEH, −4.3 on math (−12.9 to 4.3; n = 93).
>    - P3 missed, and P5's math part missed.
>    - On BBEH the setting barely changed spending, so the vendor-versus-reasoning confound stays open. No claim.
> 4. AH4 (exploratory; power 0.23).
>    - The Sol low judge's eta was 0.85 on easy items and 0.14 on hard ones (difference 0.72, 0.41 to 1.05), the direction P4 predicted.
>    - Eta is a ratio whose hard-half denominator is small, which is why the interval passes 1. No claim without a new prereg.
> 5. Width (exploratory).
>    - On BBEH a 3-vote added at most 2.6 points over one call at about 3x the dollars.
>    - On math, Sol low's 3-vote added 7.9 (35.1% to 43.0%) at $0.096 against $0.032 per item, mostly through abstentions.
>    - One Sol low call (56.2%) beat every cheaper arm and vote.
>    - Sol medium added nothing over Sol low on BBEH (60.9% vs 60.6%; P6 held).
>    - Systematic share (no interval): 0.61 overall; BBEH 0.65, near LiveBench's 0.65; math 0.43, well below MathArena's 0.73.
>    - The copula reproduces the all-wrong tail: 34.7% predicted against 34.2% observed when all five arms are wrong, against 8.2% under independence.
> 6. Verification (exploratory).

## 2026-10-26, audit T1-1: the open-weight testbed design before any GPU spend (session T1; $26.56 API spent, $0 GPU)

Numbered T1-1 so it cannot collide with E5's audits on the parallel branch. Verdict recorded verbatim:

> VERDICT: ADJUST. None of the fixes cost money. Three of the five primaries cannot support their stated decisions as written.
>
> BUDGET: API spend is $26.56 (anthropic $22.05, openai $4.50), unchanged since audit 67; the $20 pilot phase is closed. GPU spend is $0 of $100. Nothing has run, so there is no estimate-vs-actual yet (prior estimates on an H200: pilot about $4, stage 1 about $43). The tree is clean and HEAD equals origin/ow_testbed (4b37b35).
>
> FINDINGS:
> 1. [critical] T4 is decided by arithmetic, not by coupling. A judge of constant skill (d'=1) has eta 0.40, 0.61 and 0.72 when 1, 2 or 3 of its 4 candidates are right. That alone gives eta(easy) − eta(hard) of about +0.3. The judge in world.py picks a right candidate with fixed probability, so its eta does not depend on how many are right, and the flat_judge null is a near-perfect judge. The simulation therefore cannot show this. The terciles are also in-sample (/home/user/AutoAgentNetwork/autoagentnet/testbed/analysis.py:280).
> 2. [critical] T5 is circular. beta_i, U_i and the wrong-answer distribution q_i are fitted on the held-out items' own samples (/home/user/AutoAgentNetwork/autoagentnet/testbed/factor.py:192-193), the same samples the replay scores. Only alpha and V are out of sample, and T5 has no null world.
> 3. [major] The T1 primary D1 equals BF16's own vote gain on the hard third minus secondary B. It can be "supported" with zero quantization loss wherever voting fails (floor items, binary answers). Its "not supported" branch just repeats T2 on the hard third. The stated prior of 1–4 point W4 losses ignores /home/user/AutoAgentNetwork/research/lit/ow_testbed_sources.md:108 (9B W4A16 on AIME: 85.4 → 68.8).
> 4. [major] corr_gap is confounded by accuracy levels. In a pure-shift world with no shared shock it reads +0.001 to +0.015, against +0.010 to +0.024 planted. The disattenuated cross-correlation separates the two (0.998 vs 0.978).
> 5. [major] On code, vote credit pools hidden-test pass rates over all 36 configs (/home/user/AutoAgentNetwork/autoagentnet/testbed/compose.py:99), while single arms use each sample's own correctness. This biases T1–T3 on 100 of 363 items.
> 6. [major] T3's equal-cost k is unreliable:
>    - Fully cached shards get gpu_seconds = 0 and partly cached ones are undercounted (/home/user/AutoAgentNetwork/autoagentnet/testbed/sample.py:157-158).
>    - Capped pairs enter the primary.
>    - Under the random tie rule a 2-vote equals one sample, so T2's "2×W8 vote" arm is degenerate.
> 7. [major] Money (/home/user/AutoAgentNetwork/scripts/testbed_env.sh):
>    - Nothing stops a step mid-run, so the protocol's 1.5× rule is not enforced.
>    - step-hours defaults to the H100, which makes A100 guards 1.8× too low.
>    - The pilot re-estimate corrects throughput only, not tokens per call. With modestly higher token counts (thinking-off answers of 700 tokens, budgets 90–95% used) stage 1 on an H200 comes to about $56.
>    - Code grading and setup (2.4 of the 9.6 prior hours) run on the GPU clock.
>    - The per-size loop deletes checkpoints even after a break.
> 8. [minor] The H200 over the H100 is sound. The A100 prior uses the memory-bandwidth ratio, but decoding 256 requests at once is closer to compute-bound (relative speed about 0.32), which gives about $49 over about 27 h.
> 9. [minor] Protocol gaps:
>    - no testbed entry in plan.md;
>    - Kevin's GPU caps are not quoted in STEERING.md;
>    - stage 2 has no prereg decision and no audit gate;
>    - no 2-item live smoke test;
>    - the pilot uses stage-1 items and seeds.
>
> REQUIRED BEFORE NEXT PAID RUN:
> 1. T4: split eta by how many candidates are right, take difficulty from samples that are not candidates, and add a constant-skill judge null.
> 2. T5: fit on call 0, score votes of up to 5 on calls 1–5, and add a misspecified null.
> 3. T1 primary: the disattenuated gap, or the precision gap at 6 votes minus at 1 on the hard third. D1 counts only when B > 0 as well.
> 4. Score code votes by each config's own class credit.
> 5. T3: take k from the token ratio and exclude capped and cached shards.
> 6. Money: wrap each step in a timeout of 1.5× its estimate and pass --machine. Re-estimate from measured tokens and ledger hours, and preregister what to drop if over (W3 first). Grade code and install off the GPU. Delete checkpoints only after success. Add an idle watchdog; Kevin turns off Lightning auto top-up.
> 7. Recalibrate world.py to the pilot's accuracies and require power of at least 0.80. I would worry about:
>    - a false-positive rate above 0.05 under the new nulls;
>    - a 95% half-width above 3 points;
>    - a hard third near floor accuracy.
> 8. Freeze the test definitions before the pilot and add the plan.md and STEERING.md entries.
>
> DIRECTION: Do the $0 fixes, then run the pilot ($4–10) mainly as a cost and floor check. If the measured stage-1 estimate is above about $45, or BF16 accuracy on the hard third is near zero, cut W3 and the kernel run before stage 1. Stage 2 and the kernel run are there because they were planned, not because they are informative yet; gate each behind its own prereg and audit.

## 2026-10-26, audit T1-2: re-audit of the testbed design after the T1-1 fixes (session T1; $26.56 API spent, $0 GPU)

Verdict recorded verbatim:

> VERDICT: ADJUST. Every fix costs $0. Do them before the pilot, because prereg.md freezes sections 1–8 at the pilot.
>
> BUDGET: API spend is $26.56 (anthropic $22.05, openai $4.50), unchanged; the $20 pilot phase closed long ago. GPU spend is $0 of $100. Nothing has run, so there is no estimate-vs-actual yet (the pilot prior is about $4 on an H200). The tree is clean and HEAD f68628a equals origin/ow_testbed.
>
> FINDINGS:
> 1. [critical] T5 passes its own null when answer spaces are small. The baseline spreads wrong answers over 9 classes on every item (n_classes=10 in /home/user/AutoAgentNetwork/autoagentnet/testbed/analysis.py t5). I ran the misspecified world with 5 classes: the ratio was 0.51 and 0.50, "supported" in both draws, the same as the default world's 0.525. Setting a bar by simulation before data is legitimate, but this one was calibrated at only one class count.
> 2. [major] T4 is confounded by which config the candidates come from. Within one stratum m, easy units come mostly from weak configs and hard units from strong ones. A judge whose skill does not change with difficulty and depends only on candidate size (d' 3/2/1 for 2B/4B/9B) gave a gap of +0.15 [0.10, 0.21] and +0.07 [0.01, 0.13], both "supported". A constant judge gave +0.02 and −0.02.
> 3. [major] The T1 primary cannot answer T1's question. gap_persists was supported in both pure_shift draws (+0.07, +0.06), the world where W4 only lowers every item's chance of success. Choosing a primary by simulated power is legitimate before data. But its 0.94 is the prior's own confidence, so the result will hardly inform. Its decision text ("spend on precision") ignores that at equal k, W4 costs less.
> 4. [major] T3 votes with k=2. In the default world the 4096/1024 cost ratio is about 2.1, so k=2. A 2-vote equals one sample (prereg section 3), so half the primary's cells compare depth against one cheaper shallow sample. This biases T3 toward depth.
> 5. [major] GPU money can burn silently (/home/user/AutoAgentNetwork/scripts/testbed_watchdog.sh).
>    - If the shell dies mid-step, a stale step.lock disables the watchdog for good.
>    - The guard checks 1.0× the estimate while the timeout allows 1.5×, so the judge step can overshoot $55 by about $5.
> 6. [minor]
>    - pilot-report runs on the CPU machine, so throughput.json records "H100" and the H200 stage-1 guards are 10% optimistic.
>    - WATCHDOG_STAGE is never set, and the watchdog is not restarted at each boot.
>    - The estimates leave out prefill.
>    - T4's power assumes a planted gap of 0.30.
>    - T5's false-positive claim rests on 0 of 40 null draws (95% upper bound 0.09).
>
> REQUIRED BEFORE NEXT PAID RUN:
> 1. T5: make the baseline uniform over each item's answer space (the probe's observed wrong classes, or the option count). Add null worlds with 2, 5 and 10 classes, and re-set the bar on at least 100 null draws.
> 2. T4: take the gap within config × m, and add a null whose judge skill depends on the candidate's config.
> 3. T1: retitle the primary as descriptive ("the W4 deficit survives 6 votes") and narrow its decision.
> 4. T3: score the wide arm as a cost-matched mixture of the two neighbouring odd k.
> 5. Money:
>    - write the PID and start time into step.lock, and have the watchdog ignore a stale lock;
>    - guard at 1.5× the estimate;
>    - pass --machine to pilot-report;
>    - set WATCHDOG_STAGE explicitly.
> 6. Rerun power.csv, then a narrow re-audit.
>
> DIRECTION: After these fixes (a few CPU hours, $0), the pilot is the most informative step per dollar. At about $4–5 it answers what gates everything else: whether vLLM serves Qwen3.5's hybrid layers with budget forcing that works, what the real throughput and tokens per call are (so whether stage 1 fits), and whether the hard third is above its floor. T1 and T2 will mostly confirm priors; T3–T5 are where stage 1 can change a decision. If the drop rule must cut, protect the judge step first. Stage 2 stays unplanned until stage 1 shows what it would decide.

What was done about it: research/log.md, session T1 entry after audit T1-2.

>    - Panel.from_calls gives every call in an answer class the grade of the class's first member (replay.py:213-216). On code, the class is the program's outputs on the public tests (checkers.behaviour).

>    - Made-up check: Sonnet off wrong, Sonnet max and Sol low right, same public outputs. Their accuracies come out as 0, 0, 0.

> 5. [major] The 128,000 ceiling is nominal for Sol.

There, a mid-stream cut raises StreamCut, the SDK does not retry it, and it is not ledgered even though it may be billed (audit 87, item 5). openrouter, maritime_proxy and local hosts may also ignore include_usage.
> 2. [ok] No study-CD grade was read. The backfill's usecols exclude correct and answer. The map uses only TIER1_RUNS. stage_balance and patch_context replay pilot data only.

>    - As a result, sol_low vote@3 wins the ≤3 s cells by artefact.
>    - A vote's TTFAT is taken as its fastest call, but the voted answer only exists after the slowest one.
>    - The latency card shows OpenAI thinking as 0 with no flag.
> 4. [major] `held_out` in /home/user/AutoAgentNetwork/analysis/latency_map.py has three problems:
>    - Picks that break a budget on the scoring half add negative regret (5 cells, down to −2.2 pt).
>    - It uses one split only.
>    - It measures regret against the scoring half's in-sample best, which biases it upward.
>
>    Separately, single-call p95 is taken over per-item means of 3 calls. For Sol on BBEH that gives 24.6 s against a per-call 28.0 s.
> 5. [minor] measurement.md §3 says "nothing meets a p95 under 8 s", but haiku_off single is 5.65 s; it should say ≤5 s. The decode "ranges" are ranges of point estimates, not 95% intervals.
> 6. [major] Problems in /home/user/AutoAgentNetwork/research/v4/measurement/patching_prereg_draft.md:
>    - Its own n ≈ 3,900 already exceeds the 3,696-item BBEH pool, and TOST at 80% power needs about 4,300.

>    - The item slice is not checked against the sealed sets.

- Sol 仅一次调用：HTTP 200、无重试、无缓存、正常 stop；882 输入 / 39 输出 tokens。
- JSON 解析及真实 pipeline 验证成功：11 次工具操作、CPU 8.89 秒；合成验证分数 0.9583。
- Sol 账本费用 `$0.002154`；两模型总费用 `$0.0022938`。输出、usage、账本和缓存一致。
- 冻结哈希匹配，运行前代码干净；原始账本完整保留，仅追加两行；受保护模型、价格和预算配置未修改。
- 远端测试分支已确认包含第二次状态提交 `eec5598fd1ce4217239fbbd9a1c6edf866172ed0`。

这证明模型接入、解析、工具执行、计费及持久化正常；尚未验证正式恢复实验的效果或跨供应商接入。


## PHOENIX science pre-run audit SC-1 (2026-10-31, session SC)

ADJUST. Budget: $117.09 spent before the study (anthropic $89.41, openai $27.68); phase scienceclaw ($310; room
anthropic $149.72, openai $59.28).
- [major] The calibration-pilot go/no-go cannot be applied as written: a one-task slice ends "running" (rule 4);
  rule 1 allows no parse failure at n=3 although calibration records them as observations; rule 2's basis (the toy
  smoke's cost) is not request-sized. Study-pilot rule 2 would fail by design (recovery requests ~3.9k tokens).
- [major] Prereg to be frozen now, with a missing-outcome rule and a re-pilot rule.
- [minor] Pilot tasks 0, 10, 20 all use covtype folds; calibration outputs are committed by hand; rest of calibration
  in slices <= 5 tasks; batch rerun after a re-freeze would overwrite finished statuses; withdrawn balance on the 15
  evidence cases is T1 5/3, T2 3/4; BatchCaller should call caller.cache_key.
- [ok] Batch runner: caps hold and no double payment (locks around check+reserve, settle+ledger, cache). Testbed
  changes are method-neutral (thread/child CPU, hard links with atomic replace, rounding only in requests).
What was done: research/phoenix_science/prereg.md Amendment 1 (calibration and study-pilot rules, missing outcomes,
re-pilot, evidence balance, slices of <= 5), committed before any calibration output was opened; smoke logged and
estimates recorded in research/estimates.csv; fake dry runs of calibration tasks on the MiniBooNE and HIGGS folds;
plan pointer in research/plan.md.
